In 2018, during Brexit negotiations, Johnson called for Britain to leave the [Single Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Single_Market)[^cite_note-businessinsider-681] and advocated a more liberal approach to immigration than that of Prime Minister May.[^cite_note-bloomberg_20180523_boris-684] He stated many people believed that [Britain's EU membership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_membership_of_the_European_Union) had led to the suppression of British wages and said the EU was intent on creating a "superstate" that would seek to rob Britain of its sovereignty.[^cite_note-businessinsider-681] In 2019, Johnson said he would take Britain out of the EU on 31 October whether there was a trade deal in place or not.[^cite_note-theguardian_2019may26_brexit-685] Johnson also stated his opposition to a [referendum on the Brexit withdrawal agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Referendum_on_the_Brexit_withdrawal_agreement).[^cite_note-Auto4S-44-686]

On 19 August 2019, Johnson wrote a letter to the EU asking for the removal of the "backstop" accord. The president of the European Council, [Donald Tusk](648724.md), rejected the proposal.[^cite_note-kcbx_20190820-687] On 26 August 2019, Johnson said that Britain would not pay £39 billion for the withdrawal agreement were the UK to leave without a deal. The European Parliament Brexit coordinator [Guy Verhofstadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guy_Verhofstadt) said there would be no further negotiation unless the UK agreed to pay the entire sum.[^cite_note-Auto4S-45-688]

### Unionism and devolution

Johnson described himself as a "fervent and passionate [unionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unionism_in_the_United_Kingdom)".[^cite_note-bbc_50338539-689] He proposed building an [Irish Sea Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Sea_Bridge), but he later scrapped this initiative.[^cite_note-bbc_58574878-690] The devolved administrations have criticised the [Internal Market Bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_Market_Bill) for its re-centralisation of control over commerce.[^cite_note-scotsman_2967633-691]

## Public image

Main article: [Public image of Boris Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_image_of_Boris_Johnson)

Boris Johnson pulling a cow.

Johnson visiting a cattle farm in [Aberdeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeen) in 2019

Johnson has said that "humour is a utensil that you can use to sugar the pill and to get important points across".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell20113-692] He is said to have a genuine desire to be liked.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGimson2012258-693] Johnson has been described as having a light-hearted and charming persona;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell2011103-694][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECrines20131-695][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHill201631-696][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGimson2012x-697] many biographers and commentators suggest he has put significant effort into developing this version of himself.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell201191-698][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwardsIsaby200874-699][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwardsIsaby200874Purnell20111Gimson2012301-700][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell20113-692] He has also been described as heavily focused on his own interests,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell2011183-701][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGimson2012108-702][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGimson201217-703] with an often vitriolic or irresponsible way of conducting himself in private.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell20116–7-704][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGimson2012301-705][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell201137-706]

Johnson has been described as a divisive, controversial figure in British politics.[^cite_note-nbcnews_1009841-707][^cite_note-ABC3-708][^cite_note-FT-709] Supporters have praised him as witty and entertaining.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGimson201220-710] Johnson has been accused of lying or making misleading statements throughout his career,[^cite_note-theguardian_2021dec10_lies-711] and has been compared to US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md).[^cite_note-Chadwick-2016-664][^cite_note-Greenslade-2016-665][^cite_note-newsweek_439957-712][^cite_note-atlantic202110-666] He has been considered a figure with broad appeal outside of the usual Conservative support base.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwardsIsaby200847-713] Johnson's premiership has been described by historians as the most controversial and scandal-affected since that of [David Lloyd George](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Lloyd_George) about a century earlier.[^cite_note-Observer-historians-714]

## Personal life

Since Johnson was born in New York City to British parents, he held [British-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British-American) [dual citizenship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_citizenship). In 2015, he agreed to pay [capital gains tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_gains_tax) to the [US tax authorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_Revenue_Service) on a property that he inherited in the UK.[^cite_note-telegraph-20141120-715][^cite_note-guardian-20141120-716][^cite_note-bbc-20150122-717] He renounced his US citizenship the following year.[^cite_note-Auto4S-46-718][^cite_note-guardian-8–february-7][^cite_note-federalregister_20170209_2017-719] Johnson has knowledge of French, Italian, German, Spanish,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell2011109-71] [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin) and [Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek),[^cite_note-irishtimes_625047-720] frequently making [classical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classics) allusions in his newspaper columns and his speeches.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell201191-698]

[Sonia Purnell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonia_Purnell) wrote in 2011 that Johnson was a "highly evasive figure" when it came to his personal life,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell20111-721] who remained detached from others and who had few intimate friends.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell201115-722] Among friends and family, Johnson is more commonly known as Al (short for his forename Alexander), rather than Boris.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwardsIsaby200843Purnell20111Gimson2012xiii-723]

In 2007, Johnson said he had smoked [cannabis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannabis) before he went to university.[^cite_note-oxfordmail_1446705-724] He has also said he had used [cocaine](7701.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwardsIsaby2008144–145-725] Johnson partakes in cycling, tennis and [pilates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilates), and returned to road running in 2023.[^cite_note-sky_12897306-726] He was considered [obese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obese) in 2018 and [overweight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overweight) in 2020, and has spoken of making efforts to lose weight.[^cite_note-Mohdin-2020-727][^cite_note-sky_12037000-728]

Johnson previously owned a £1.3 million buy-to-let townhouse in [Camberwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camberwell), south London. According to [HM Land Registry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HM_Land_Registry) documents, he bought the four-bedroom property with his then-girlfriend [Carrie Symonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrie_Symonds) in July 2019. The register of MPs' interests states that Johnson had a rental income of at least £10,000 a year.[^cite_note-independent_2075095-729] In 2023, Johnson and Symonds bought [Brightwell Manor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brightwell_Manor) in [Brightwell-cum-Sotwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brightwell-cum-Sotwell), Oxfordshire.[^cite_note-Auto4S-47-730]

### Religion



Johnson speaking at [Westminster Abbey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westminster_Abbey)'s [Commonwealth Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Day) service, 2020

Johnson was baptised [Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic) and later [confirmed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confirmed) in the [Church of England](5955.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell201148-35] but has said that his faith "comes and goes"[^cite_note-theguardian_2008apr26_whowouldjesusvotefor-731] and that he is not a serious practising Christian.[^cite_note-telegraph_11378107-732] In 2020 his son Wilfred was baptised Catholic.[^cite_note-Auto4S-48-733] Johnson and Symonds married in a Catholic ceremony at [Westminster Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westminster_Cathedral) on 29 May 2021.[^cite_note-BBC20210530-734] Since he was baptised Catholic, but his previous weddings were not conferred by the Catholic Church, the Catholic Church considered them putatively invalid.[^cite_note-co_20210529_boris_how-735][^cite_note-Auto4S-49-736]

Johnson holds ancient Greek statesman [Pericles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pericles) as a personal hero.[^cite_note-thetimes_20190723_pericles-737][^cite_note-reuters_idUSKCN1UI1LV-738] According to Johnson's biographer, Andrew Gimson, regarding ancient Greek and [Roman polytheism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_polytheism): "it is clear that \[Johnson] is inspired by the Romans, and even more by the Greeks, and repelled by the [early Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_Christians)".[^cite_note-economist_20190727_boris-739] Johnson views [secular humanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secular_humanism) positively and sees it as owing more to the [classical world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_world) than to Christian thinking.[^cite_note-spectator_20190804-740] In 2021 Johnson was asked if he held pre-Christian beliefs, which he denied, saying, "Christianity is a superb ethical system and I would count myself as a kind of very, very bad Christian... Christianity makes a lot of sense to me."[^cite_note-theatlantic_619010-741]

### Relationships and children

| Children of Boris Johnson |
| --- |
| by [Marina Wheeler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marina_Wheeler) |
| Lara Lettice Johnson-Wheeler (b. 1993) Milo Arthur Johnson (b. 1995) Cassia Peaches Johnson (b. 1997) Theodore Apollo Johnson (b. 1999) |
| by Helen Macintyre |
| Stephanie Macintyre (b. 2009) |
| by [Carrie Symonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrie_Symonds) |
| Wilfred Lawrie Nicholas Johnson (b. 2020) Romy Iris Charlotte Johnson (b. 2021)  Frank Alfred Odysseus Johnson (b. 2023)  Poppy Eliza Josephine Johnson (b. 2025) |
| [^cite_note-hellomagazine_202112131288-742][^cite_note-evoke_20211209_entertainment-743][^cite_note-son23-744][^cite_note-IND24052025-745] |



Johnson with his then-fiancée [Carrie Symonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrie_Symonds) on [Commonwealth Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Day), 2020

Johnson and [Allegra Mostyn-Owen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegra_Mostyn-Owen) married on 5 September 1987;[^cite_note-\:0-746] they had no children together.[^cite_note-bordercountiesadvertizer_20892889-747][^cite_note-bbc_65650532-748][^cite_note-sampson-749][^cite_note-Auto4S-50-750] On 26 April 1993, the couple were granted a divorce or [annulment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annulment),[^cite_note-753] which Mostyn-Owen sought due to Johnson's affair with barrister [Marina Wheeler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marina_Wheeler), daughter of journalist [Charles Wheeler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Wheeler_\(journalist\)).[^cite_note-theguardian_2004aug29_comment-754][^cite_note-marriagedateperIndy-755] On 8 May 1993, twelve days after the end of his marriage to Mostyn-Owen, he married Wheeler,[^cite_note-co_20210530_boris-756] who gave birth to their first child five weeks later.[^cite_note-marriagedateperIndy-755][^cite_note-backboris_20080406-757] They have four children together: Lara (born 1993), Milo (born 1995), Cassia (born 1997) and Theodore (born 1999).[^cite_note-bbc_7376621-758][^cite_note-belfasttelegraph_39167307-759]

Between 2000 and 2004, Johnson had an affair with *[Spectator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Spectator)* columnist [Petronella Wyatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petronella_Wyatt), resulting in a terminated pregnancy and a [miscarriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miscarriage).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwardsIsaby200847Purnell2011257–263Gimson2012162–173,_209–218-157] In April 2006, the *[News of the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_of_the_World)* alleged that he was having an affair with *[Times Higher Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Times_Higher_Education)* journalist Anna Fazackerley.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell2011b-760] The pair did not comment; Fazackerley resigned after her editor questioned her about the allegations, and Johnson secured her a job as director of a higher education think tank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell2011b-760][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell2011296–300Gimson2012255–257-168][^cite_note-Barber-2008-169] In 2009, he fathered a daughter, Stephanie, with arts consultant Helen Macintyre, who subsequently lost a legal action to prevent publication of her daughter's paternity.[^cite_note-Telegraph210513-200][^cite_note-Guardian130521-761][^cite_note-thetimes_20130522-762] Johnson has declined to discuss his children when asked how many he had in subsequent interviews.[^cite_note-bbc_50433134-763][^cite_note-independent_9225756-764] American entrepreneur [Jennifer Arcuri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Arcuri) said that she had an affair with him from 2012 to 2016.[^cite_note-mirror_23809260-765][^cite_note-independent_1823529-766] In early 2018, he began an affair with Carrie Symonds, then the Conservative Party's director of communications.[^cite_note-independent_2105238-767][^cite_note-theguardian_2020nov29_carrie-768] In September 2018, following media reports about the affair, Johnson and Wheeler confirmed that they had separated and were divorcing.[^cite_note-bbc-7–september-769] Johnson was subsequently reported to be sharing a flat with Symonds.[^cite_note-theguardian_2019jun21_police-770]

After Johnson became prime minister in July 2019, he and Symonds moved into a flat above 11 Downing Street, the first time a prime minister had lived in Downing Street with an unmarried partner.[^cite_note-theguardian_2019jul29_boris-771][^cite_note-ITV_22_June_2019-772] In February 2020, his divorce from Wheeler was finalised, making him the first prime minister to get divorced while in office since 1769.[^cite_note-guardian200218-86] Later that month, Symonds announced that she and Johnson had become engaged in late 2019 and were expecting their first child.[^cite_note-BBC51691434-773] She gave birth to their son Wilfred in April 2020.[^cite_note-standard_4430206-774][^cite_note-Guardian200429-775][^cite_note-bbc_52469079-776] She suffered a miscarriage in early 2021.[^cite_note-bbc_58042146-777] On 29 May 2021, Johnson and Symonds married at Westminster Cathedral.[^cite_note-BBC20210530-734][^cite_note-cnn_20210529_world-778] In September 2021, Johnson claimed in a US television interview that he then had six children, the first time he had stated the number,[^cite_note-sky_12413590-779][^cite_note-theguardian_2021sep21_boris-780] although this has not been officially confirmed.[^cite_note-Auto4S-51-781] He and Symonds subsequently had three more children, a daughter Romy (born December 2021),[^cite_note-bbc_59686354-782][^cite_note-sky_12490631-783] a son Frank (born July 2023),[^cite_note-son23-744] and a daughter Poppy (born May 2025).[^cite_note-sky_13373914-784] The couple purchased Brightwell Manor, a 400 year-old [Grade II-listed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grade_II) country house, near [Wallingford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wallingford,_Oxfordshire) in [Oxfordshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxfordshire) in 2023, for around £3.8 million.[^cite_note-785]

### Family and ancestors



Johnson and his younger brother Leo in 2013

Johnson is the eldest of the four children of [Stanley Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Johnson_\(writer\)), a former [Conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_\(UK\)) [member of the European Parliament](258909.md), and the painter [Charlotte Johnson Wahl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlotte_Johnson_Wahl) (née Fawcett),[^cite_note-telegraph.co.uk-5] the daughter of Sir [James Fawcett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Fawcett), president of the [European Commission of Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Commission_of_Human_Rights).[^cite_note-Auto4S-52-786] His younger siblings are [Rachel Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rachel_Johnson), a writer and journalist, Leo Johnson, a broadcaster,[^cite_note-Auto4S-53-787] and [Jo Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo_Johnson), ex-minister of state and former Conservative MP for [Orpington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orpington_\(UK_Parliament_constituency\)), who resigned from his brother's government in September 2019[^cite_note-bbc2-405] and is now a member of the [House of Lords](13658.md). Johnson's stepmother, Jenny, the second wife of his father Stanley, is the stepdaughter of [Teddy Sieff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teddy_Sieff), the former chairman of [Marks & Spencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marks_&_Spencer).[^cite_note-theguardian_2008may14_5-788] Having been a member of the Conservatives between 2008 and 2011, Rachel Johnson joined the Liberal Democrats in 2017.[^cite_note-theguardian_2017apr27_rachel-789] She stood as a candidate for [Change UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change_UK) in the [2019 European Elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_European_Parliament_election_in_the_United_Kingdom).[^cite_note-theguardian_2019apr23_change-790] Johnson also has two half-siblings, [Julia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Johnson) and Maximilian, through his father's later marriage to Jennifer Kidd.[^cite_note-telegraph_3636228-791][^cite_note-Auto4S-54-792]

Johnson's paternal grandfather, Wilfred Johnson, was an RAF pilot in [Coastal Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_Command) during the Second World War.[^cite_note-co_20200502_story-793] Wilfred Johnson's father was the [Ottoman](22278.md) [Interior Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_the_Interior_\(Ottoman_Empire\)) and journalist [Ali Kemal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Kemal). Ali Kemal's father was a [Turk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_people) while his mother was a [Circassian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circassians) reputedly of [slave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circassian_beauties) origin.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEdwardsIsaby200844Purnell201119–20Gimson20125–7,_1957-794][^cite_note-hurriyet_9229598-795][^cite_note-aksam_510362-796] His other paternal ancestry includes English, German and French; one of his German ancestors was said to be the [illegitimate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illegitimate) daughter of [Prince Paul of Württemberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_Paul_of_W%C3%BCrttemberg) and thus a descendant of [George II of Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_II_of_Great_Britain),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell201121Gimson201210-797] which was later confirmed on an episode of *[Who Do You Think You Are?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who_Do_You_Think_You_Are%3F_\(British_TV_series\))*[^cite_note-youtube_20181004-798]

Johnson's mother is the granddaughter of [Elias Avery Lowe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elias_Avery_Lowe), a [palaeographer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palaeographer) and a [Russian Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Jewish) immigrant to the US,[^cite_note-thejc1-799] and [Pennsylvania](23332.md)-born [Helen Tracy Lowe-Porter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helen_Tracy_Lowe-Porter), a translator of [Thomas Mann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Mann).[^cite_note-haaretz_5421852-800] Referring to his varied ancestry, Johnson has described himself as a "one-man melting pot".[^cite_note-theguardian_2007jul17_localgovernment-801] Johnson was given the middle name "[Boris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boris_\(given_name\))" after a [White Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_%C3%A9migr%C3%A9) émigré named Boris Litwin, who was a friend of his parents.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPurnell201111Gimson20122-4] Johnson's third given name, de Pfeffel, refers to the name of his great-grandmother Marie-Louise [de Pfeffel's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pfeffel_family) family.
